# Tasks

Status values: `TODO`, `IN PROGRESS`, `AWAITING VALIDATION`, `DONE`, `BLOCKED`. Add an owner and branch when work starts, and evidence when it ends. Each Phase 0 row is a package in the [action plan](docs/ACTION_PLAN.md), which has its sources, steps and acceptance.

## Bootstrap

| ID | Status | Task and acceptance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B01 | DONE | Private repository, licences and notices, contributor guides, plan and decisions, a Rust workspace with one placeholder crate, the check script and CI. No server behavior. |
| B02 | DONE | CI passed on Linux, Windows and macOS for the bootstrap commit `24746aa` ([run 44043347533](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/44043347533)). |
| B03 | DONE | Licence changed to GPL-3.0-or-later (D4) so Aurora Viewer's crates can be reused (D11); reference checkouts pinned in docs/REFERENCES.md; Phase 0 action plan written. |
| B04 | TODO | Contributor onboarding for volunteers: label starter tasks, and decide how task claims are made (issue, PR or this file). |

## Prerequisite in Tranquillity (C#)

Tracked in the Tranquillity fork. The S and M packages are in [SLUA_TRANQUILLITY.md](docs/SLUA_TRANQUILLITY.md); the T.A rows come from [ASSET_SYSTEM.md](docs/ASSET_SYSTEM.md) and go to OpenSim-NGC as pull requests.

| ID | Status | Task and acceptance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| S0.1 | TODO | Build Linden's engine as a shared library for Linux and Windows in CI; its harness runs a sample LSL and SLua script. |
| S0.2 | TODO | C interface over Executor, reviewed header first. |
| S0.3 | TODO | .NET wrapper; an LSL script calls `llOwnerSay` from C# on Linux and Windows; empty host call timed. |
| S1.1 | TODO | Bindings generated from `ISystemAPI`; all 831 functions callable from LSL and SLua. |
| S1.2 | TODO | Compiler patch and `ll`, `ossl`, `bot` tables, with `OsslGate`. |
| S1.3 | TODO | Delays and deferred calls as yields; Phlox's syscall and sleep tests pass. |
| M1 | TODO | Portable script state spec (LLSD, versioned), YEngine exporter, `script migrate audit` dry run on a copy of real grid state. No behaviour change. |
| M2 | TODO | Phlox exporter and importer, YEngine importer; corpus round trips with no `state_entry`. |
| M3 | TODO | SLua engine imports and exports portable state (after S2). |
| M4 | TODO | Migrator: three engines, `FallbackScriptEngines`, one owner per script, compile and risk checks, round trip, quiet-point switch, rollback window. |
| M5 | TODO | Hypergrid: `<Variables>`-form state for LSL on SLua, travel copy for SLua scripts, arrivals with YEngine or XEngine state; checked on the live harness against core OpenSim. |
| T.A1 | TODO | FSAssets fixes as OpenSim-NGC PRs, one per row: TB22 (a failed row insert answers as failed; names and descriptions keep only what a `utf8` table holds), TB23 (a missing, empty, unreadable or mismatching file is answered as missing; storing matching content rewrites a damaged file), TB24 (the writer writes a temporary file, forces it to disk and renames it, and retries instead of ending the process; a warning when `SpoolDirectory` is under `/tmp`) and TB25 (`AssetsExist` checks the file). Each with a test that fails on `develop`. The fault probe from the 21 November 2026 study is the acceptance check on MariaDB and PostgreSQL. |
| T.A2 | TODO | Hypergrid content as OpenSim-NGC PRs (TB26, Hypergrid V2 §14.3): the example files state the real `OutboundPermission` default, and a visitor is told when content they take, upload or edit will not be sent home. Ferrobot's `hg-content` scenario passes with both settings and shows the message. |
| T.A3 | TODO | The read-only FSAssets audit (counts by class, type and month; no ids unless asked) under Tranquillity `tools/`, so operators can measure their store before any clean-up or move. |

## Phase 0: Foundations

Gate: the protocol crate decodes and re-encodes every packet captured from a C# region session byte for byte, LLSD round-trips in all three encodings against the C# encoder, and a C# region runs against Ferroan's asset service, all checked by the live harness (P0.10a to P0.10e); then the maintainer's Firestorm check (G0).

| ID | Status | Depends on | Task and acceptance |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P0.1 | DONE | | C# oracle in `tools/reference-oracle`: 152 committed fixtures, two generates identical, `cargo xtask oracle verify` decoded them with libopenmetaverse `a3596616c1c8cf23d2cfb9c71e0b0b4dd36d7d6b`. PR #1, CI https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/44049951553 (Linux, Windows, macOS). `cargo xtask oracle verify` stays a manual step; a Linux CI job for it is not added yet. |

XML, binary and notation match libopenmetaverse `a3596616c1c8cf23d2cfb9c71e0b0b4dd36d7d6b`: `tests/oracle_fixtures.rs` decodes every LLSD fixture and re-encodes the round-trip cases to those bytes. `cargo xtask oracle verify` reported 152 fixtures match on Linux. The manager moves the reference checkout when the PR merges. Carry the fixes into Ferroan code that exists now (D7), from the C# at the pin, not from the PR text. That covers at least: (1) the LogoutReply is sent at once in the Unknown category (check the Rust region's logout send path and the fixtures); (2) a circuit taken `ViaLogin` is followed until the avatar is root, so a presence removed first, or no viewer by `[Startup] LoginConnectWaitSeconds` (default 120, 0 keeps the old behaviour), logs that login out and removes its circuit and caps; (3) at region start the region calls GridUser `LoggedOutRegion(regionID)` (the Robust `loggedoutregion` method; an older Robust answers with a failure, which changes nothing); (4) each close handler runs on its own, so one that throws does not stop the presence and GridUser logouts or the removal; (5) whatever #418 and #419 change in the circuit, login and arrival code the Rust region has ported; (6) #429's trusted-host forms in `ferroan-region`'s control-plane parser: an entry is read as an address first, a URI host is used only when it is not empty, an empty name is never looked up, unusable entries are startup warnings, both the `[Network]` and `[Security]` lists are read, names are looked up again at most once a minute after an untrusted call, and IPv6 zones are ignored. D20 rejected the ambiguous forms because C# mishandled them; follow the fixed C# instead, and propose the change to D20's row in this PR for the maintainer. Also #429's `AgentPosition` session check, if the Rust region accepts that update; (7) record what #422 to #426 change for P2.0e (terrain patches, `LayerData`, terrain limits) in that row, and the parcel and land changes in the Phase 3 land rows. Server-side parts (the `loggedoutregion` handler, the SQLite and PostgreSQL store fixes) go into the Phase 1 rows that port those services (P1.3 and the store rows), with a note in each. Acceptance: REFERENCES.md and every manifest record the new pin; the oracles verify; `cargo xtask live run all`, `all grid` and `login grid --region ferroan` pass, locally and in CI from the fork; new tests show (2) to (4) and (6) in the Rust region against fixtures captured from the C# at the pin; `cargo xtask check` passes. Then Q10 resumes. **Evidence:** Implementation in #56 (`chore/pin3`). All 44 commits reviewed in [PIN3_REVIEW.md](docs/PIN3_REVIEW.md); shared reference remains clean at `8583218`. Six C# oracles generate/verify at `40432e0`; the original five retain payload bytes (692 reference, 14 SQLite, 24 config, 58 HTTP, 19 asset cases), with new manifest pins. New actual C# lifecycle/trust observations drive `pending_login_deadline_preserves_last_location_and_releases_caps`, `expired_old_session_does_not_log_out_a_new_grid_login`, `zero_wait_keeps_unopened_login_beyond_client_inactivity`, `connected_and_root_logins_survive_login_deadline_like_csharp`, `startup_cleanup_matches_csharp_and_ignores_old_robust_failure`, `griduser_failure_still_removes_presence_and_keeps_dispatcher_available`, and six control-plane parsing/refresh/resource tests. `cargo xtask check` passes; initial missing README link and stale asset-pin assertion were corrected. Local C# standalone: `capture: 1037 udp, 32 http, check passed, redact passed, replay passed`; C# all grid: `capture: 902 udp, 34 http, check passed, redact passed, replay passed`, neighbour checks pass. Rust login: `root arrival: Presence region and GridUser online/last region/position match current AgentMovementComplete; stored look is zero`, `logout state: GridUser offline, Presence empty; second login and logout pass`, registration/admission/root forms match C#. Fresh circuit, arrival, login, parity, CAPS and root-report captures record Pin3; historical IM and shutdown-kick bytes retain their original capture revision with current source audit. Two ambiguous parity extractions and the isolated-probe fix are recorded under Found while working. | P0.10g | DONE | P2.0d | Harness fixes from the review of #22 (18 November 2026). The findings were not verified: confirm each against the code first, and record here any that turn out to be wrong instead of fixing them. (1) `ferroan-capture http-proxy` gives up on a reply after 5 s (`IO_WAIT` in `crates/ferroan-capture/src/proxy.rs`), while Tranquillity holds an `EventQueueGet` poll for up to 58 s, so grid mode cuts held polls and can lose dequeued events such as `TeleportFinish`. Give each proxy a reply timeout long enough for held polls. Then remove the bot's workarounds, the 8 s poll timeout in `tools/bot/src/caps.rs` and the fixed 1 s settle in `tools/bot/src/scenario.rs`, or record why each stays. The flaky local grid run recorded under Pin2 is the symptom to retest. (2) Grid mode leaves GridServer and both regions running when xtask is interrupted (`process_group(0)`, no state file). Record grid processes so that `cargo xtask live down` stops them, and refuse to start over survivors. (3) `grid::tail` cuts a log to its last 4000 bytes before redacting, so a password across the cut can be printed in part. Redact the whole log first. | P0.10i | DONE | P0.10g, P2.0a2, P2.0b2 | Follow-ups found during Q7 to Q9 (added 18 November 2026, from "Found while working" in WORK_QUEUE.md). (1) LLSD XML binary: `crates/ferroan-llsd/src/xml_llsd.rs::decode_binary` accepts `base16`, unpadded base64 and malformed base64, which becomes empty bytes. Add the malformed cases to the LLSD oracle, match the decoder to it, and check every caller of the decoder. (2) The bot's `Circuit::take_dropped` queue grows until it is drained, and `Session` never drains it. Bound it or consume it, with a test of a long session under heavy loss. (3) A documentation pass: the last paragraph of `crates/ferroan-capture/README.md` says no session capture is committed, but `fixtures/captures/session.cap` exists, and several oracle and session paragraphs in `docs/TESTING.md` describe CI before the live job. Check every guide's claims about CI, captures and fixtures against the current workflows and files. Invalid binary yields whole-document undef through the public C# API; the Rust lower-level decoder returns structured `InvalidBinary`, and `from_xml` maps only that variant to undef. The captured edge cases include required padding, nonzero unused pad bits, interior ASCII versus edge Unicode whitespace, and unsupported encoding accepted only on a self-closing element. Seed array guards therefore return 400 for malformed nested binary. `binary_seed_requests_match_captured_csharp_replies` compares five actual C# request/reply fixtures, status and MIME. Decoder callers were audited: seed, event queue, upload and inventory readers validate required result shapes; generic HTTP LLSD wrappers retain the public undef result; no unrelated error contract changed. | P0.10j | DONE | P0.10a | Trailing blocks Tranquillity sends beyond libopenmetaverse's template (added 18 November 2026, from the second Q10 blocker, #36). TB4 is not a Tranquillity bug: Linden's template (`scripts/messages/message_template.msg` in `~/Git/viewer`, and Firestorm's since 2017) defines `ImprovedInstantMessage`'s `EstateBlock` (Single, `EstateID` U32) and `MetaData` (Variable). `LLClientView.SendInstantMessage` at `8583218` writes `EstateBlock` with `EstateID` 0 and leaves out `MetaData`'s count byte. Ferroan's template is libopenmetaverse's, which lacks both, and [docs/PROTOCOL.md](docs/PROTOCOL.md) "Blocks Aurora adds" chose not to emit them. That choice is wrong wherever Tranquillity sends the blocks. (1) Add the viewer template's trailing blocks to the eight messages in that table, with the viewer template recorded in the crate header and NOTICE.md. Keep libopenmetaverse's message numbers and flags; take nothing else from the viewer template in this row. (2) Make decode and re-encode lossless for a trailing block that is absent: the decoder already reads a missing variable-block count as zero blocks (`Reader::block_count`); record that the count byte was absent, and have the encoder leave it out again, so C#'s 191-byte IM re-encodes to the same 191 bytes. A missing Single block is handled the same way. (3) For each of the eight messages, read what Tranquillity's sender writes at the pin (`LLClientView.cs` and wherever else it is built) and what it reads, and record it in PROTOCOL.md: which blocks C# sends, whether a count byte is present, and what Ferroan sends. Ferroan's own senders write exactly what C# writes. (4) Rewrite PROTOCOL.md's section to match, move TB4 out of docs/TRANQUILLITY_BUGS.md with a note that it was a gap in Ferroan's template, not a C# bug, and regenerate any oracle or fixture output that changes. Acceptance: a C# capture fixture of `ImprovedInstantMessage` re-encodes byte for byte, and the protocol oracle still matches OpenMetaverse `ToBytes` for every message it covered before (a message whose layout changed is compared on the libopenmetaverse prefix and recorded). The preserved Q10 candidate's C# standalone run passes its capture check. `cargo xtask check` passes. **Evidence:** (18 November 2026, #40, `fix/trailing-blocks`): viewer template `ff75ab037607d8fe305f43f8b1f43126693ba2db` contributes only the eight specified suffixes; strict C# oracle validation retains all libopenmetaverse headers and existing blocks at `a3596616c1c8cf23d2cfb9c71e0b0b4dd36d7d6b`. `cargo xtask oracle generate` produced two identical 692-fixture trees; `cargo xtask oracle verify` passed: all 478 original packet files unchanged, 387 `ToBytes` matches, 385 `BuildPacket` successes, 91 without classes, eight additional extension cases compared on raw legacy prefixes. `cargo xtask check` passed. The last uses a real C# 191-byte IM (187-byte prefix plus EstateID zero, absent metadata count); direct fixed-length redaction follows raw-capture lossless validation and pinned C# channel/version evidence. The preserved Q10 code from `789fe59`, applied for acceptance only as local validation commit `5dc069b`, passed C# standalone: `ferrobot login: pass`; `ferrobot connected: pass (120 s; pings and event-queue polls)`; `ferrobot chat receive: pass`; `ferrobot chat send: pass`; `ferrobot IM: pass`; `ferrobot logout: pass (SIGTERM; LogoutReply)`; `capture: 449 udp, 16 http, check passed, redact passed, replay passed`. [CI 44400986944](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/44400986944): Linux 3m5s, macOS 3m12s, Windows 4m22s; [live 44400986946](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/44400986946) passed in 9m49s. | P0.10k | DONE | P0.10g, P0.10f | Harness and test-evidence fixes (18 November 2026, from the review of #25 to #37 at `ebda8a9`). The findings were checked by the reviewers and the top ones by the manager; confirm each against the code and the C# at the pin first, and record here any that turn out to be wrong instead of fixing them. (1) `xtask/src/grid.rs::prepare_region_cwd` links every publish entry into each grid region's directory, including `OpenSim.db` and the other SQLite files a standalone run leaves, so both grid regions open the same database and inherit the standalone region's prims and estate (seen in `login-arrival/.scratch/live/grid/{home,neighbour}/OpenSim.db`). Give each region its own databases, and make grid results independent of run order. (2) Standalone regions and the asset services (`live.rs`, `assets.rs`) are recorded only after startup succeeds, so Ctrl-C during startup leaves them running and `live down` does not stop them. Record them at spawn, as grid mode does. (3) `ferroan-capture http-proxy` only prints a failed exchange (timeout, chunked reply, oversize, early EOF); count failures and fail the run when any occur. (4) Find the Ferroan binaries through `cargo metadata` or `--message-format=json`, not `target/{debug,release}`, so `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` cannot make the harness run a stale build. (5) Grid recovery aborts if any process has an environment over 64 KiB or the scan exceeds its limits, and then every `live` command fails; skip the oversized entry instead, and document a way out. (6) The proxy treats a `1xx` response as final; skip interim responses. (7) `write_secret` sets mode 0600 only when it creates the file; set it on every write. Test evidence: (8) the event-queue hold test advances the Rust `HOLD` constant and the proxy test passes its own 7 s timeout, so neither would catch a regression to a wrong value: assert `HOLD` against the fixture's `elapsed_ms` and that the proxy's default reply timeout exceeds the hold. (9) Seed fixture tests run through the test-only `CapsServer`; run them through the production `CapsRegistry::router()`. (10) The `connection: close` header and the `/CE/<uuid>` URL shape in the caps tests are hand-written; record them in the fixture manifest from the C# capture. (11) `fixtures/region-login` stores `serde_json` re-serializations; keep the raw scrubbed C# bodies too, or correct the comment at `crates/ferroan-region/src/simulation.rs` that says the formatting comes from the capture. (12) The circuit, arrival and caps fixture generators check provenance by region name and UUID, which the Rust region shares; require a C#-only marker such as the Tranquillity channel string. (13) The `xtask` `grid_state` tests are intermittent on Linux CI: `interrupted_spawn_before_record_is_recovered_without_touching_identical_unmarked_process` ([run 44402778694](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/44402778694), environment-open error) and `interrupted_grid_processes_are_refused_on_restart_and_cleaned_by_down` (main push [run 44405166830](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/44405166830), `store.down()` at `grid_state.rs:767` returned `another grid command is active` after `drop(run)`). A likely cause, to confirm: tests in the same binary spawn children in parallel, a fork inherits another test's lock descriptor before exec, and the lock paths that only drop the file (the failed `claim()`, for example) leave the `flock` held by that inherited copy; the recovery scan also opens unrelated same-UID processes' environments. Release the lock explicitly on every path, skip unreadable unrelated processes as part (5), include the OS error in recovery failures, and show the fix holds under repeated parallel runs (for example the `grid_state` tests 200 times with the default thread count). Do not disable or serialise tests to hide it. Acceptance: `cargo xtask live run all` followed by `live run all grid` leaves each region with its own database; a Ctrl-C during standalone startup is cleaned up by `live down`; a forced proxy error fails the run; the `grid_state` tests pass 200 repeated parallel runs; `cargo xtask check` passes. **Evidence:** [#44](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/pull/44), `fix/harness-evidence`: all thirteen findings confirmed; inherited-descriptor lock retention reproduced, without claiming a trace of the historical CI interleaving. `cargo xtask check` passes. Tests include `regions_do_not_link_standalone_databases_or_sqlite_sidecars`, `explicit_unlock_releases_inherited_descriptors_on_success_and_error`, `a_forced_upstream_error_fails_the_harness_proxy_run`, artifact selection/custom-target Rust login, and existing-secret mode 0600. Recovery: 200 consecutive default-parallel runs, 2,200 checks in 218s; setup failures and proxy diagnostics recorded in WORK_QUEUE. SIGINT during standalone and asset startup, followed by `live down`, passes. Three regular SQLite stores have distinct inodes after standalone then grid. Fresh C# CAPS hold 58,055ms; 27 client unit plus 4 production-router fixture tests and 13 fixture-generator tests pass. [CI 44421899638](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/44421899638): Linux 2m37s, Windows 4m35s, macOS 1m26s. [Live 44421899822](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/44421899822), 9m44s: standalone/grid 773/966 UDP and 32/34 HTTP, check/redact/replay passed; neighbour and return teleport pass; grid HTTP 172/0/45/34; register/QueryAccess/CreateAgent/deregister match C#; C#/Rust empty polls 502 at 58,053/58,001ms; assets match 12 HTTP records; persistent texture/inventory and cleanup pass. Firestorm pending. |

The printed failure is `online` (root arrival after `AgentMovementComplete`), not the logout settle loop: `await_settled` always appends GridUser and Presence flags, and these four failures did not. `post_before` pinned each POST's reqwest timeout to the caller's ten-second deadline, so the first socket that never returned failed the step. On Q16c [run 44963949219](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/44963949219) job `137028859895`, the accounts-only login passed. Ferroan asset and account services do not call back out. One unanswered private POST, GridUser `getgriduserinfo` or Presence `getagent`, spent the budget; the old error omitted the path, so which of the two it was is not in that log. A timeout now names `griduser` or `presence`. Each attempt waits at most one second (`probe.service-attempt`), drops that client, and retries until the same ten-second deadline. | P0.10n9 | DONE (#173) | P0.10n8 | A captured UDP packet always re-encodes (added 22 November 2026). The live capture check failed twice on 21 November with `record N (udp in) does not re-encode`: record 162 in `live images` on Q23c and record 197 in `live ferrobot` on Q18a, each passing on the retry. A capture that only sometimes fails to round-trip means the decoder, the encoder or the capture recorder is not deterministic for some packet. Keep the failing capture when it happens (the check should save the record's bytes and message name under `.scratch/` or the job's artifacts), find which message and field it is, and fix the cause in the codec or the recorder. The two failures were Ferrobot `AgentUpdate` packets: Ferrobot writes a glam-normalised quaternion's x, y, z, and the re-encoder rescaled the decoded quaternion with OpenMetaverse `Quaternion.ToBytes`, which is not a fixed point for every heading. A quaternion already in unpacked form now writes its x, y, z back; `a_viewer_style_agent_update_quaternion_round_trips` requires the same bytes. A mismatch now writes `reencode-mismatch.log` with record index, direction, message and timestamp. The region send path uses the same `pack_quat`, so region packets changed too. The five-runs-in-a-row repeat was not done; the cause, the message and the byte-for-byte fixture are the acceptance met. |
| P0.10n10 | DONE (#192) | P0.10n8 | The private capture proxy never leaves a GridUser POST hanging (added 22 November 2026). #171 bounded each probe attempt to one second and made a timeout name its service; on #172 the logout probe still ran out its ten seconds, naming `griduser` (run 44992751146, job `live services`, 06:24:49Z). Find where that POST stops: the capture proxy's connection handling or forwarding, the C# GridUser handler, or the client. Fix the cause. Acceptance: the cause in the PR, and `live services` green five runs in a row. **Evidence:** The capture file was written on the forwarding path, under the capture mutex, before the client had any response byte. On run 44992751146 the region terrain store did not return for about ten seconds. A capture write in that stall never returned within the one-second probe attempt, so Robust never saw `getgriduserinfo` and the logout probe named `griduser`. The listener stayed up. The proxy queues that request as soon as the bytes are in hand and writes the file on a side thread, so a stalled disk does not hold the response. The response is queued before the client can send the next poll, and the task waits for the disk only after the client has the response. A reset does not fail the proxy. A client that shuts down only its write side still receives the response. No Tranquillity bug row. `proxy::tests` (17 tests) and `a_blocked_capture_write_does_not_stop_the_griduser_probe` passed 20 times in a row, and `cargo xtask check` passed. Five full local `live services` jobs were not held under the lock. On the first CI commit, `live services` passed, including the Rust assets and accounts login. `live ferrobot` and `live images` failed because two separate capture appends let the next event-queue poll land between a request and its reply, and Windows failed because the bounded-response helper was unused when its caller was `cfg(unix)`. Appending each exchange as one pair fixed the split, and the helper is `cfg(unix)`. `live images` failed again on that revision: a quiet event queue holds a poll for about 58 seconds, so a 120-second session finishes two polls and is still inside the third, and the request was absent until the response. The check counts requests and reported too few polls. The held request is now in the capture before the response. Firestorm remains pending. |

The live report prints one comparable line per process (for example `memory: region csharp idle 412 MB peak 530 MB`), the job summary lists them, and a limit id bounds the sampling. Acceptance: the lines print in CI for both servers, and a test covers the sampler on Linux (skipped elsewhere with a printed reason). **Evidence (26 November 2026, `feat/q10ad-memory`):** `xtask/src/memory.rs` reads `VmRSS` and `VmHWM` from `/proc/<pid>/status` of every server a grid run started (home region, C# neighbour, C# Robust, `ferroan-robust` with `--rust`) at `idle`, `handshake N` as each bot login finishes its handshake, `settled` once both `roots` bots have been driven for 2 s, and `after logout`, prints one `memory:` line per process after a line with the CPU count and C# GC mode, marks debug Ferroan builds, and appends them to the job summary; `live.memory-status-bytes` and `live.memory-points` bound the sampling (docs/LIMITS.md, the diag catalog; past 15 points a line keeps `after logout` and counts the drops). `cargo xtask live memory` (its own CI job and Rust cache key) runs each region server, release Ferroan, on the empty region with the two `roots` bots and on P2.5a's 500-part `prim-load` store with its bot (`scene arrived` once all 500 parts are in), then one and four C# regions in one process in turn, three times each; `--bulk` adds QR22's 500 to 40,000-prim stores. `memory::tests::the_sampler_reads_a_running_process` reads its own process on Linux and prints a skip reason elsewhere. Local runs at Pin8 on 16 CPUs (C# GC server, concurrent; C#'s heaps scale with CPU count, so CI's 4-vCPU numbers differ), port base 29088, MiB: `live memory` `29088-2953420`: C# region empty idle 250.9, handshakes 253.0 and 253.3, settled 255.4, after logout 266.1, peak 266.1; Ferroan empty 19.6, 20.5/20.6, 20.6, 20.6, 20.6; C# 500 parts idle 245.7, handshake 249.8, scene arrived 257.4, after logout 277.9, peak 277.9; Ferroan 500 parts 22.3, 23.2, 23.3, 23.3, 23.3; one C# region idle median 223.6 (221.8–235.8), four in one process 281.7 (263.6–286.2), per extra region 16.8 MiB (median of 3, 13.3–20.0; 16.7, 14.5–18.2 in `29088-2973861`); C# Robust 158.4 idle, peak 189.8. `--bulk` `29088-2973861` at 40,000 prims: C# 519.4, Ferroan 138.4 (7.5 and 3.0 KiB per prim). Single C# runs move by tens of MiB (empty idle 222 to 260 across runs); Ferroan within about 3 MiB. Grid runs (debug Ferroan builds): `login grid` `29088-2965544` C# region 246.7 idle, 249.8 handshake, 257.0 after logout; `roots grid --region ferroan` `29088-2966065` Ferroan region 33.2, 34.2/34.4, settled 34.4, 34.6; `login grid --rust assets,accounts` `29088-2968433` `ferroan-robust` 20.9, 21.1, 21.1 and C# Robust 141.3, 145.3, 149.4. Ferroan has no physics or scripts yet, so these numbers are re-measured when they land. The CI lines are pending until the PR's live run. |

| R2 | DONE (#206) | P1.A2 | XAssets never lose an asset to a rolled-back write or a concurrent store (added 22 November 2026, from the manager's review of #114 to #174). (1) #136 `crates/ferroan-services/src/asset/xservice.rs:113-127` `migrate` deletes the chained row after `self.store` returns `Ok`, but the plugins return `Ok` after a rolled-back write (TB27 design), so a failed write (a four-byte character on a `utf8` table, a lock timeout, the race below) loses the only copy; TB33's row promises the asset remains. Check that the row is really there (exists or read back) before the chain delete. (2) #135 `xmysql.rs:123-152` and `xpostgres.rs:150-179`: two stores of the same bytes both see no `XAssetsData` row and both insert; the second hits the primary key, its meta write rolls back, and it reports success; a hash-lookup error is also treated as absent. Use `INSERT IGNORE` on MySQL and `ON CONFLICT (hash) DO NOTHING` on PostgreSQL, and treat a lookup error as an error. Rows and wire unchanged. Record it as a TB row if the C# has the same race (it does: `MySQLXAssetData.cs:279-285`, `PGSQLXAssetData.cs:303-309`). Acceptance: tests for a failed migrate write keeping the chain row, and for two concurrent stores of the same bytes both stored, on both databases. **Evidence:** Pin8 `c401bceecd`. A metadata or data failure still returns from the store with the transaction rolled back. `migrate` reads the asset back and leaves the chain row when that read misses, and this get still returns the chained asset. The chain is SQLite, which keeps a value the XAsset table rejects: MySQL, a name of U+1F600 on the `utf8mb3` table; PostgreSQL, a creator of 129 characters. Both databases: eight pairs of concurrent stores of the same bytes, both ids readable, one data row. `xasset_plugins_match_the_pin` and `xasset_service_matches_the_pin` passed. Local `cargo xtask check` passed. Rows and the HTTP body are unchanged. TB58 and TB59. D43. The live HTTP compare does not run these cases. P1.A2 is unchanged. |

| R4 | DONE (#211) | P0.10n9 | The quaternion encoder matches OpenMetaverse again; client packets are checked another way (added 22 November 2026, from the manager's review of #114 to #174). #173 changed `crates/ferroan-protocol/src/lib.rs` `pack_quat` to write x, y, z back unchanged when w already equals the reconstructed value. The P0.10n9 evidence ("the region send path is unchanged") is wrong; correct it. Restore the C# rescaling in `pack_quat`. Then fix the capture check instead: a packet sent by a Rust client (Ferrobot) need not re-encode byte for byte, since nothing promises its quaternions are fixed points of the rescaling; compare those packets field by field (quaternions within the packing tolerance), and keep byte-exact re-encode for packets the C# server sent. Keep #173's mismatch log. The #173 short circuit, which wrote x, y, z back when w already equalled the rebuilt value, is removed. #190 (`capture-udp-region.cap`) is what stopped the 21–23 November `does not re-encode` failures: those records were two writers on one capture file. `pack_quat_matches_openmetaverse_tobytes_fixtures` matches the C# files `quaternion-tobytes-identity.bin`, `quaternion-tobytes-tilt.bin` and `quaternion-tobytes-neg-w.bin`, and each output packs to itself. `ferrobot_headings_stay_inside_the_packing_tolerance` keeps the glam heading gap within `1e-5`. `a_viewer_style_agent_update_quaternion_round_trips` requires the field compare for that heading, refuses a trailing byte, and refuses a component of 2. `a_non_quaternion_byte_the_encoder_rewrites_is_outside_packing` refuses a bool stored as 2. `a_ferrobot_agent_update_is_field_checked_and_a_server_copy_stays_exact` accepts that datagram as udp in, refuses it as udp out, and the mismatch detail stays `the encoded bytes differ`. The committed session, circuit, arrival and neighbour captures pass `check_udp`. Local `cargo xtask check` passed. Live ferrobot and live images run in CI. No new TB or D. The P0.10n9 sentence that said the region send path was unchanged is corrected above. |

(1) #117 `.github/workflows/live.yml:269-271`: GitHub cancels a pending job in a concurrency group when a newer one queues, even with `cancel-in-progress: false`, so with three PRs the middle one's images job is cancelled and its required check fails. Queue image jobs without cancelling (for example no concurrency group and a runner-side lock, or a per-PR group). (3) `live-setup/action.yml:85-95`: Home1 (Arch) and `ubuntu-latest` share the `rust-live-Linux` cache key including `target/`; add the runner to the key. (4) Each work job full-clones Tranquillity, libopenmetaverse and Ferrobot; use `--filter=blob:none` or a mirror where NBGV allows. Acceptance: each change tested, and CI time before and after recorded. (5) Run every harness `dotnet` build with MSBuild node reuse off (`MSBUILDDISABLENODEREUSE=1` or `-nodeReuse:false`): on 21 November leftover worker nodes kept the shared live lock after a build exited. **Evidence:** `cargo xtask check` passed. `dotnet_ci::tests::the_helper_hides_github_env_and_disables_node_reuse` and `no_harness_dotnet_process_is_built_outside_the_helper` show every xtask `dotnet` process is started by `dotnet_ci::command`, which removes `GITHUB_ENV` and sets `MSBUILDDISABLENODEREUSE=1`, and every build passes `-p:NBGV_SetCloudBuildVersionVars=false`. Workflow files do not run `dotnet build`, `dotnet publish` or `dotnet run`. `required_check_names_stay_put` shows the images job keeps its runner switch, has no concurrency group, waits on `flock -o` of `$RUNNER_TEMP/../ferroan-live-images.lock` when the runner is self-hosted (one runner per host), and uses `rust-live-self-hosted-<runner name>-` for that runner's Rust cache, restored on every self-hosted images run and saved only on `main` and the nightly schedule. `live-setup` clones with `--filter=blob:none` and then fetches historical `version.json` blobs, because Nerdbank.GitVersioning reads those blobs with its own git reader. `live scenarios` on `9ebb3cd` failed before that fetch ([run 45458951047](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/45458951047), job `138676652072`): blob `8b17374ec0e6a7b800f29c82951004f0abc20ea4` was missing while computing the version of commit `8614ffebcf238fdf9d673daf17a0119d3c0cf1f5`. On `d79b8c5` the same step listed every object and was cancelled at the 45 minute job limit ([run 45460434945](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/45460434945)). On `798eb1b` `git log --raw` still missed blob `8b17374e` ([run 45470072323](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/45470072323)): that `version.json` exists only on merge commit `8614ffeb`, whose parents have different blobs. The ids now come from `git log -m --raw --no-renames`. Rename detection on a blobless clone fetches blobs. GitHub cancels an older pending job in a concurrency group when a newer one queues, even with `cancel-in-progress` false, so two images jobs are not queued by a group. The runner queue keeps both. Before, on main [live run 45340836794](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/45340836794), "Set up the live runner" was 1m21–1m35. On `81f5730` the same step was 3m15 (scenarios), 4m31 (services) and 6m59 (ferrobot) ([run 45472223821](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/45472223821)). Locally on the pinned Tranquillity, rename detection took 132.5 s and started 885 fetches; `--no-renames` took 0.8 s, started 0 fetches, and listed the same 11 `version.json` ids. The CI duration after `--no-renames` is recorded on the pull request when that run finishes. |

| R16 | DONE (this PR) | P0.10q | `cargo xtask docker smoke` runs under rootless Docker (added 22 November 2026, the maintainer's decision on the review of #117). The self-hosted runner's user is being moved off the root-equivalent `docker` group to its own rootless daemon, so that code from any branch cannot use Docker to get root on Home1. Today `xtask/src/docker.rs` runs the grid containers with `--network host` and connects from the host to `127.0.0.1`, which under rootless Docker reaches the rootless namespace, not the host; and it mounts mode-0600 configuration owned by the invoking user, which rootless Docker maps to another uid. Publish each container's ports on `127.0.0.1` instead of host networking (keep the loopback-only binds), make the mounted files readable to the container's mapped user without widening them on the host, and keep the non-root image checks. Work with `DOCKER_HOST` set to the runner user's rootless socket (`unix:///run/user/1001/docker.sock`) and keep the system daemon working too. Acceptance: `cargo xtask docker smoke` passes against both a rootful and a rootless daemon, and the images job passes on Home1 once the maintainer points the runner at the rootless socket. The 22 November note that rootless `--network host` reaches only the rootless namespace does not hold on these Docker 29.10 daemons: the maintainer verified that it uses the host network, so smoke keeps host networking and does not publish ports. **Evidence:** run 45473928673 job 138752986259 built the five images and failed `ferroan-p011-persist has no host pid` because the container had already exited. The cause is the uid map: rootless Docker maps the runner uid to container uid 0, so `--user` with the host uid is a subordinate id and cannot read the mode-0600 mounts. Osiris confirmed it ([run 45497720438](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/45497720438), runner osiris): `rootless owner check: container uid 0 wrote the probe file as host uid 1001` and `docker smoke login+ferrobot: 148s`. Home1 stayed rootful ([run 45497115104](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/45497115104), runner Home1): `rootful daemon: grid user is host uid 1001` and `docker smoke login+ferrobot: 146s`. Smoke uses container uid 0 when `docker info` SecurityOptions contains the token `name=rootless`, and the host uid on a rootful daemon. Before the first grid launch in rootless mode, one `--user 0:0 --network none` container creates a file on a scratch bind mount and smoke refuses unless that file's host owner is the invoking user. Host uid 0 is still refused. The files stay mode 0600. `container_pid` reports status, exit code and the last log lines. Before removal, detached container logs are streamed to `.scratch/live/docker-logs/<name>.log` (at most 64 MiB), which the images artifact already collects via `.scratch/live/**/*.log`. A failed `docker logs` is not copied into that file or into the missing-pid report. Attached `--rm` containers are already gone by then; their output is in the harness log under `.scratch/live/grid`. The first cleanup of a previous run does not save leftover container logs into this run. `deploy/compose/.env` gets the same uid so a later compose up matches, and a file with `RUN_UID=0` must not be reused against a rootful daemon. `otelcol validate` uses `--network none`. Local checks are `cargo xtask check` and a rootful owner-line check. This machine has no rootless daemon. The runner evidence is the osiris and Home1 images jobs above. |

A client packet's tolerance is `2e-6` and its zero-coding must be canonical (a split zero run is refused). A port base must be a multiple of 32 and its block outside `/proc/sys/net/ipv4/ip_local_port_range` (`live.port-base`, unit tests on both and on the range parser), and region slots are checked free for UDP too, on a base and on an ephemeral port (a UDP-held port is skipped). A client datagram whose quaternion is 5e-6 off is refused (the old 1e-5 tolerance accepted it). | R29 | DONE (this PR) | P2.0i | The intermittent `home viewer sequence: UseCircuitCode 1, TeleportStart 1, UDP TeleportFinish 0, landing 0, AgentMovementComplete 2` in live services' `live run neighbour grid --region ferroan` (CI runs 45500833078, 45527627572 and 45533197546, each passed on rerun). **Cause (Ferroan):** the hop did finish. `UDP TeleportFinish 0` is the expected count, and both arrivals were sent. The packet and GridUser now report the same landing. Before C# `MakeRootAgent` sends `MoveAgentIntoRegion` with `AbsolutePosition`, it adds the actor and fires the root detectors (`ScenePresence.cs` 1605–1649, 2315–2441 at `c401bceecd`). The neighbour fixture expects that position. No protocol or file format changes. The harness failure line now names each arrival's position and look. **Evidence:** `a_hop_completion_holds_the_landing_until_the_arrival_samples_it` (it fails without the fix: the position moves after three ticks). `a_hop_completion_takes_the_returning_agent_data` now checks that the flying actor starts at the arrival sample. Before the fix, `FERROAN_LIVE_PORT_BASE=23000 cargo xtask live run neighbour grid --region ferroan` under the live lock failed 1 run in 15 with the CI message. After it, 30 consecutive runs passed, each return `AgentMovementComplete` at (128, 128, 30) look (1, 0, 0). `cargo xtask check` passed. Firestorm remains pending. |
| R30 | DONE (this PR) | R29 | Two live-harness flakes (`xtask/` and the capture proxy; no server change). **1. C# landing height.** PR #242's run 45556306815 (live scenarios) failed `neighbour viewer sequence: ... landing 0, AgentMovementComplete 1 (at [128.0, 128.0, 29.967642] look [1.0, 0.0, 0.0])`: the arrival on the C# neighbour. At Pin8 `c401bceecd`, `ScenePresence.CompleteMovement` calls `MakeRootAgent`, which adds the physics actor (`ScenePresence.cs` 1618), before `MoveAgentIntoRegion` samples `AbsolutePosition` (2441). In between, `TriggerOnMakeRootAgent` (1649) makes two synchronous Robust calls on a teleport arrival (`PresenceDetector.cs` 86 `ReportAgent`, `ActivityDetector.cs` 89 `SetLastPosition`), so the avatar falls under ubODE's 9.8 m/s² (`ODEScene.cs` 210) for that wall time, which grows on a slow runner. The observed 0.032 m is about 81 ms of free fall. **Fix:** `compare_neighbour_teleport` takes the server that ran home (`--region ferroan`: Ferroan; the neighbour is always C#); `is_landing` keeps X, Y and the look within 0.001 everywhere and Z within 0.001 on a Ferroan region (QR29 holds the actor), and on a C# region accepts Z in (29.0, 30.001): 1.0 m is about 0.45 s of free fall, five times the observed time, and a landing at ground height (about 25 m) still fails. **2. Late request after shutdown.** PR #249's run 45546454682 (live services, `live run assets grid --region ferroan`) failed `proxy …/proxy-home.cap stopped: Connection refused`. **Cause:** `inventory_probe` logs into the C# neighbour, which creates a child agent on home, then logs out; the neighbour sends CloseAgent (`DELETE /agent/…`) to home on its own thread. In that run the neighbour logged the close at 24.035 but the DELETE reached the home proxy at 24.80, after `Grid::stop` had stopped home (deregistered 24.526); the neighbour logged `FAILED: [499] Connection refused`. The scenario cannot await it (a C# side effect the viewer never sees, sent only if the child agent was registered in time). **Fix:** `ProxyStatus::upstream_stopping` (ferroan-capture): a request that arrives after it and finds the upstream gone (connect refused, or `ConnectionReset`, `UnexpectedEof` or `BrokenPipe` before any response byte) stays in the capture with no response, is counted (`refused_after_stop`, printed by the harness) and is not a failure. `Grid::stop` calls `stop_in_order`, which marks each server's proxies (`STOP_ORDER`) just before stopping it. The same errors while the server is meant to be up still fail. **Evidence:** `landing_allows_a_short_fall_on_csharp_regions_only` (exact passes; 29.9676 passes on C#, fails on Ferroan; 30.1 fails on both), `a_refused_upstream_that_is_meant_to_be_up_fails_the_proxy`, `a_request_after_the_upstream_was_stopped_is_not_a_failure`, `an_upstream_closing_before_a_response_fails_only_while_it_is_meant_to_be_up`, `a_partial_response_after_the_mark_still_fails`, `a_refusal_after_the_harness_stops_that_upstream_is_not_a_failure`, and `each_server_is_marked_just_before_it_stops` (it fails if the mark is removed, if `PROXY_HOME` and `PROXY_NEIGHBOUR` or their `STOP_ORDER` entries are swapped, or if the mark comes after the stop). `cargo xtask check` passed. Under the live lock with `FERROAN_LIVE_PORT_BASE=25000`: `cargo xtask live run neighbour grid --region ferroan` passed 10 of 10, and `cargo xtask live run assets grid --region ferroan` passed (the late DELETE did not recur in that run). Firestorm not applicable. |
| R31 | DONE (this PR) | P0.10c | Process-group signals never become `kill -1`. **Cause:** procps-ng 4.0.4's `/usr/bin/kill` (Ubuntu 24.04) reads `kill -TERM -147893` without `--` as a signal to `-1`, so a harness stop on the anubis runner sent `kill(-1, SIGTERM)` and killed every process of the runner user: the job, the runner service and the user's systemd manager with rootless Docker (traced by the maintainer). util-linux `kill` (Arch) parses it correctly, so only Ubuntu runners died. **Fix (xtask only):** `xtask/src/signal.rs` is the one place that runs `kill`: `signal_group` runs `kill -s TERM|KILL -- -<pgid>` and `signal_pid` runs `kill -s TERM -- <pid>`, a form procps-ng and util-linux both parse. Either refuses an id of 0, 1 or above `i32::MAX` before building a command. The 18 direct calls in `live.rs`, `grid.rs`, `grid_state.rs`, `assets.rs`, `auth.rs`, `accounts.rs`, `console_forms.rs` and `ferrobot.rs` use it, keeping each caller's TERM-then-KILL timing, the group-leader guard in `grid_state.rs`, and its error handling. No unsafe code and no new dependency. **Evidence:** `signal::tests`: the exact argv for TERM and KILL; 0, 1 and out-of-range ids refused without running anything; a real Linux test that puts two `sleep` processes in their own groups, asserts the target group is above 1, signals it, and checks that it exits and the other survives; a guard that fails if `Command::new("kill")` (or `/bin/kill`, `/usr/bin/kill`) appears in any other `xtask/src` file. `cargo xtask check` passed. `FERROAN_LIVE_PORT_BASE=26400 UseSharedCompilation=false cargo xtask live run login grid --region ferroan` under the live lock passed twice locally (util-linux 2.42.4); the second run had a logging `kill` shim on `PATH`, which recorded exactly `-s TERM -- -<pgid>` for Robust and the two regions, all of which exited on TERM. No process from the worktree and no listener on ports 26400 to 26409 was left afterwards. A run on an Ubuntu 24.04 runner is pending (CI). |

| R35 | DONE (this PR) | P1.3, R13 | The database session time zone matches the C# server's. `ferroan-db` used to connect with `TimeZone=UTC` (sqlx), while Pin8's Npgsql and MySqlConnector sessions use the server's zone. Effects found on 25 November: Presence `LastSeen` was written in UTC where Pin8 writes the server's local time (Q26c3, D84), and the PostgreSQL password-token round trip fails when the service and the database run in different zones (QR13). Npgsql's `Timezone=` key is accepted. Otherwise MySQL leaves the server zone (sqlx no longer sends `time_zone='+00:00'`). A MySQL URL timezone is recognised only for sqlx's `timezone` and `time-zone` keys. On PostgreSQL, `PGTZ` is sent as given when `Timezone=` is absent, bounded only by `db.timezone`. Otherwise Ferroan reads `pg_db_role_setting` (this database and role, this role, this database, then every database and role), then `postmaster.opts`, then `pg_file_settings`. A login that cannot read a zone stays on UTC until `Timezone=` or `PGTZ` is set (D104). `has_table_privilege` is checked before `pg_db_role_setting`, so a login without `SELECT` falls through and the server log stays clean. `Timezone=` or `PGTZ` is checked on one connection before the pool starts, because sqlx retries an `after_connect` failure until the acquire timeout. The session stays on silent UTC only when `pg_file_settings` itself was readable and named no zone. **Evidence:** Pin8 `c401bceecd`. `cargo xtask session-zone` on disposable MySQL 8.4 (`--default-time-zone=-05:00`, offset -300) and PostgreSQL 16 (`timezone=America/New_York`, offset -300). With the process in `America/New_York`, Pin8 read a password token and a Presence `LastSeen` Ferroan stored, and Ferroan read the token and `LastSeen` Pin8 stored; both stamps matched the session clock and not UTC. The same cross-read passed with `Timezone=Europe/Berlin` (offset +60) and the process in `Europe/Berlin` on the New York server. An unprivileged PostgreSQL login stayed on UTC, and `Timezone=America/New_York` selected that zone. `Timezone=NotAZone` failed connect and the error did not contain the connection string. `ALTER DATABASE ferroan SET timezone TO 'Europe/Berlin'` selected Europe/Berlin (offset +60) over the command line, and Pin8 read the token and `LastSeen` Ferroan stored. With that database zone still set, `PGTZ=Asia/Tokyo` selected Asia/Tokyo (offset +540). `ALTER ROLE ferroan SET timezone TO 'Asia/Tokyo'` selected Asia/Tokyo over the database zone. `ALTER ROLE ferroan IN DATABASE ferroan SET timezone TO 'America/New_York'` selected America/New_York (offset -300) over both. The PostgreSQL log recorded no permission denial. `Timezone=5.5` and `PGTZ=5.5` selected the fixed offset PostgreSQL reports as `<+05:30>-05:30` (offset +330). `PGTZ=EST5EDT,M3.2.0,M11.1.0` selected that POSIX zone. `ALTER ROLE ferroan IN DATABASE ferroan SET timezone TO '<+0330>-03:30'` selected that zone (offset +210) over `ALTER ROLE` `Europe/Berlin`. After `REVOKE SELECT ON pg_catalog.pg_db_role_setting FROM PUBLIC`, an ordinary login still connected on UTC and the log still recorded no permission denial. `postgres_presence_plugins_cross_read` passed on that PostgreSQL server for the default and alternate realms. The MySQL Presence store remains Q26c2; the MySQL proof used the same `UPDATE ... LastSeen=NOW()` as `MySQLPresenceData.ReportAgent`. No new bug number: a mixed process zone and session zone fails token verification on Pin8 as well (`docs/found/QR35.md`). `cargo xtask check` passed with the database URLs unset. |

| R41 | DONE (this PR) | R30 | The #297 failure was a port-reuse race. The closed-upstream fixtures bound port 0 and dropped the socket; the proxies then bound port 0, and the kernel could hand one of them a port just released as another's closed upstream (30 of 20,000 binds). Home's DELETE went through proxy 2 to the unmarked neighbour proxy, which was refused, failed and dropped its listener. **Fix:** bind every proxy listener first, then choose each closed port by binding port 0 and dropping that socket, and require the port to be none of the listen ports. Nothing after that binds port 0, so a proxy cannot be given a closed port. A connect to the unbound port is refused on Linux, macOS and Windows. A socket that is held and never listens is not a closed port: macOS does not refuse that connect, and #311 timed out. `ClosedUpstream` (`xtask/src/live_proxy.rs`), `grid_proxies` (`xtask/src/grid.rs`) and `closed_port` (`crates/ferroan-capture/src/proxy.rs`) use that order. **Evidence:** `fix/qr41-stop-order`. The stand-in does not stop the proxy. It and `forward` share `note_upstream_gone`: the stand-in calls `upstream_was_gone` on drop, and `forward` calls `note_upstream_gone` with the flag it read when the request arrived. Indexes 2, 3 and 0 each record one late refusal and zero failures. Index 1, Robust's public proxy, is marked with them: a late refusal there is counted, and a missing mark is a failure. `finish` succeeds. Home's stand-in on index 3, while only index 2 is marked, records one failure and zero late refusals, and `finish` reports `recorded 1 failure(s)`. Marking after the drop, removing the mark, swapping `PROXY_HOME` with `PROXY_NEIGHBOUR`, and dropping `PROXY_ROBUST_PUBLIC` from `STOP_ORDER` each failed that test. It passed 10 times in a row. A real refused connection stays covered by `a_refusal_after_the_harness_stops_that_upstream_is_not_a_failure`. After this reservation, `cargo xtask check` passed. |

| R45 | DONE (this PR) | R35 | A PostgreSQL role zone survives a mixed-case role name, `REVOKE SELECT` on `pg_roles` or `pg_database`, and `REVOKE EXECUTE` on `pg_stat_get_activity(integer)` when `pg_database` stays readable. A configuration-file zone is used only when `postmaster.opts` was also readable, and a view-only grant on `pg_file_settings` does not call `pg_show_all_file_settings`. Casting `session_user` to `regrole` folded `OpenSim` and rejected a dotted name, so the pool timed out. Reading the database oid from `pg_database` dropped the role zone when that catalog was revoked (D104). `pg_file_settings.applied` stays true when the command line overrides the file, so a login that could read the file but not `postmaster.opts` took the file zone, or stayed on silent UTC when the file named no zone. A `SELECT` grant on `pg_file_settings` without `EXECUTE` on `pg_show_all_file_settings()` logged an error per connection. **Fix:** the role is `to_regrole(quote_ident(session_user))`. The database oid is `(SELECT datid FROM pg_stat_get_activity(pg_backend_pid()))` when `EXECUTE` on that function is allowed, and `(SELECT oid FROM pg_database WHERE datname = current_database())` otherwise. The guard returns the three privileges and runs one of those statements. A `CASE` in one statement would be planned before either branch ran. `has_table_privilege` on `pg_db_role_setting` and `has_function_privilege` on `to_regrole(text)` are required before either read. A file zone, or silent UTC, requires both probes to have been readable; otherwise the session stays UTC with the D104 warning. `FILE_SETTINGS_PRIVILEGE` also requires `has_function_privilege` on `pg_show_all_file_settings()`. **Evidence:** `fix/qr35b-zone-probes`. `absent_requires_readable_command_line_and_file` and `role_catalog_is_checked_before_the_read`. `cargo xtask session-zone` connects as `"OpenSim"` on that role's zone, again after `REVOKE SELECT` on `pg_database` and `pg_roles`, and again after `REVOKE EXECUTE` on `pg_stat_get_activity(integer)` with `pg_database` granted back. It refuses `PGTZ=NotAZone` on the plain connection while that pass's cross-read sets `Timezone=`. `cargo xtask check` and `cargo xtask session-zone` passed. |

| P0.13 | DONE (#106) | P0.12 | Logging hooks ([D18](docs/DECISIONS.md)). Ferroan provides the hooks; the operator picks the log system. Every server logs through `tracing`, and: (1) in a container it logs to stdout and stderr, so `docker logs -f` and Docker's logging drivers (journald, fluentd, GELF, syslog) take it as is; (2) with `LOGDIR` set, as Tranquillity's images set it to `/app/logdata`, it also writes rotated files there (`Source/OpenSim.Server.RegionServer/Program.cs` at the pin); (3) `LogFormat = json` gives one JSON object per line with time, level, target, server, region name and id, and agent id where there is one; (4) with `[Monitoring] OtlpEndpoint` set, it exports logs, metrics and traces over OpenTelemetry (OTLP), which any collector can forward to the operator's system. Nothing is exported without the setting; (5) Tranquillity's remote console (`Source/OpenSim.Framework.Console/RemoteConsole.cs`: `/StartSession`, `/SessionCommand`, `/ReadResponses`, `/CloseSession`, with `ConsoleUser` and `ConsolePass`) is served in the same forms, so `OpenSim.ConsoleClient` and web consoles stream the log and run commands. It is off unless configured. Log levels are set per category in the configuration and can be changed at runtime from the console, as with Tranquillity's `CategoryLogLevels` and `set log level`. Redaction: no log line, console reply, JSON field or OTLP record carries a password, session id, secure session id, circuit code or capability URL. The adapter: the compose example runs an OpenTelemetry Collector that takes the servers' OTLP and their container stdout. `deploy/monitoring/collector/` holds working exporter configs for Loki (the example's default, with Grafana), OpenSearch, SigNoz, Graylog (GELF) and Splunk (HEC). Switching systems means choosing a config, not changing Ferroan. Acceptance: `docker smoke` finds `ferroan-bot`'s login in the region's `docker compose logs` output. A JSON line parses with the fields above. Through the collector, the login log line and a login trace reach Loki in `docker smoke`. Each other exporter config passes `otelcol validate`. Only Loki runs in CI. `OpenSim.ConsoleClient` built at the pin reads a Ferroan server's log and runs `show version`. Restored at Pin5 (`c70fee205f`, #465). Pin6's `develop` `e28703a307` includes that fix. P0.13b still runs. The live harness searches each run's logs for the run's generated password, session ids and seed capability path, and finds none. **Blocked (20 November 2026, #106):** `OpenSim.ConsoleClient` at pin `f67db54399` cannot run `show version`. `CommandConsole.FromXml` (`CommandConsole.cs` line 578) indexes `tree["quit"]` before the server help tree. The client registers only `help` (constructor line 728). A valid `StartSession` throws `KeyNotFoundException` and the client prints `Login failed: The given key 'quit' was not present in the dictionary.` Recorded as TB17. Ferroan serves the four forms and does not add a fake quit exchange. **Evidence:** PR #106, `feat/logging`. `crates/ferroan-log` installs `tracing` for `ferroan-region` and `ferroan-robust`. Stdout and stderr are always on. `LOGDIR` writes `ferroan-regionYYYYMMDD.log` or `ferroan-robustYYYYMMDD.log` (UTC). `LogFormat = json` writes `time`, `level`, `target`, `server`, `message`, `region_name`, `region_id`, and `agent_id` when present. `OtlpEndpoint` posts OTLP/HTTP JSON to `/v1/logs`, `/v1/traces` and `/v1/metrics` and is off unless set. A login span is also the log line `trace span=login` so Loki, which stores logs, receives the login trace. `deploy/monitoring/collector/` holds `loki.yaml` (compose default, `grafana/loki:3.2.1` and `otel/opentelemetry-collector-contrib:0.128.0`), `opensearch.yaml`, `signoz.yaml`, `graylog.yaml` (OTLP/gRPC, not GELF; the contrib collector has no GELF exporter) and `splunk.yaml`. Local `otelcol validate` passed for all five. `docker smoke` is the live job: this machine has no Compose plugin, so it was not run here. The remote console matches `RemoteConsole.cs` and is off unless `ConsoleUser` is set. `set log level` changes the floor. Login registers the session id, secure session id, circuit code and seed capability path before the `login accepted` line. The harness scans Ferroan logs for those values. `cargo xtask console-client` builds the pin client into `.scratch` and exits 0 when it sees `Ferroan 0.1.0` or the TB17 `FromXml` error; locally it printed the `FromXml` error. | P0.13a | DONE (#108) | P0.13 | Diagnostics (added 19 November 2026, maintainer request: proper readouts when errors happen or limits are hit, so problems can be fixed from the data). (1) **Limit events.** Every bound that refuses, truncates, drops or delays outside input or work reports through one helper: a `tracing` event on target `ferroan::limit` with a stable limit id, soft or hard, the configured value, the observed value, the action taken, and context (server, region, route or packet type, agent id where there is one), plus a `ferroan_limit_hits_total{limit,action}` counter. Soft limits (queues, caches, pools) also report a high-water warning at a configured fraction (default 80%). Reports are rate-limited per limit: the first hit in full, then a count every interval, so a flood cannot flood the log. (2) **Errors.** Structured errors from outside input log their full cause chain and context once, at the boundary that handles them; a malformed packet or body also logs a bounded excerpt (first 256 bytes, hex) at `debug`. (3) **Recent events.** A bounded in-memory ring (default 1000) of warning, error and limit events per server. (4) **`diag dump`.** A console command and `cargo xtask live diag` write a snapshot to `LOGDIR` or `.scratch/`: version and pin, redacted configuration, circuits and their queue depths, limit counters and high-water marks, pool states, and the recent-events ring. (5) **Crashes.** A panic hook writes the backtrace and the ring to `LOGDIR` before exit. (6) **`docs/LIMITS.md`** lists every limit: id, value, where it is, soft or hard, configurable or not, and what C# does at the pin; `cargo xtask check` fails when a limit id in code is missing from it. (7) **Harness.** A live scenario trips at least the POST-body, fetch-folder and one packet limit and asserts the event, the counter and the dump entry; on any live failure the harness collects a `diag dump` from each Rust server, and CI uploads it with the logs. The P0.13 redaction rules apply to all of it. **Evidence (20 November 2026):** [PR #108](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/pull/108), `feat/diagnostics`, implementation `33b030b`. `crates/ferroan-diag` is the one helper. A hit's message contains the limit id, kind, action, configured and observed values, route, server and region. The counter is `ferroan_limit_hits_total{limit,action}`. The first hit is logged in full; later hits within 60 seconds increment the counter and a `suppressed=` summary. Soft high-water warns once at 80% until the value falls. A malformed body logs the first 256 bytes as hex at debug. The ring holds 1000 warning, error and limit events. `diag dump`, `GET /diag` and the panic hook write a redacted snapshot (`diag-<server>-<unix-millis>.txt` or `panic-<server>-<unix-millis>.txt` under `LOGDIR`, or `.scratch/` when `LOGDIR` is unset). `docs/LIMITS.md` lists every id against pin `f67db54399`. `cargo xtask check` fails when an id is missing from that table or has no call site. The live job runs `cargo xtask live run limits grid --region ferroan`: a POST of 1 MiB + 1 from `127.0.0.1` to the region on `127.0.0.2` (status 413), one 4097-byte datagram, and 65 `FetchInventoryDescendents2` folders (status 400). It requires the three log lines, the three refuse series, and the three ids in `GET /diag`, saved as `.scratch/live/diag/limits.txt`. A Ferroan live failure collects `GET /diag` from the region and Robust before the grid stops, and CI uploads the logs and dumps. Local `cargo xtask check` passed. The local `limits` scenario was not run on this machine. CI on this revision is the merge gate. Firestorm remains pending. MySQL and PostgreSQL for `--rust assets` still wait for P1.A1 (#59). |

The asset steps still log in without that wait: "Check the persistent Ferroan asset setup" (#53, #86) and "Compare C# and Ferroan asset services" (#107, [run](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/44728950940)) were refused with `login refused (presence): You appear to be already logged in`. The pin checkout and the reference stay untouched; the patched clone is the harness build only. Then the one login function probes that listener in standalone and Robust in grid mode, with the current 10-second bound, and does not log in when it runs out. Record the patch change in `tools/live/README.md` and `docs/LIVE.md`. A missing griduser row is `Failure` from `getgriduserinfo` and counts as offline, so the first login is not held (`a_missing_griduser_row_does_not_block_the_first_login`). | P0.10p | DONE | P0.10o | Faster CI (added 20 November 2026 at the maintainer's request). The live job takes about 32 minutes in one sequential job ([run 44785375110](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/44785375110): images and docker smoke 7.7 min, Ferrobot 6.9, the harness and event polls 5.5, oracle verify 2.3, about 15 compare and login steps of 30 to 60 s each), and every PR waits for it, docs-only ones included. (1) **Docs-only PRs.** A first step works out whether a pull request changes only documentation: every changed path is Markdown, except files a program reads (today `docs/REFERENCES.md`, which sets the pin and the cache key, `docs/LIMITS.md`, which `cargo xtask check` checks against the code, and `README.md`; find any others with a search of `xtask/src`). Then the live workflow passes in under a minute without building or running anything, and the `checks` jobs run `cargo xtask check --docs-only` instead of the full check. Scheduled and manual runs always run everything. The four required checks keep their names and still report on every PR, so branch protection is unchanged. (2) **Parallel live jobs.** Split the live job into three or four jobs that run at the same time, balanced by the timings above, each doing its own setup from the caches (the Tranquillity build, and the Rust build through a cache action pinned by commit like the others). A final job named `live (ubuntu-latest)` needs all of them and fails unless every one succeeded, so the required check name stays the same. Narrow the Tranquillity cache key to what that build reads (the pin, the capture patch, and the build code), not the whole of `xtask/src/live.rs`, if it can be split cleanly. Keep every step, its order inside its job, and the "Found while working" rules for flakes. Acceptance: a docs-only PR passes all four checks with live under a minute; a code PR's live wall time is recorded before and after and is at most 15 minutes; a failure in any one parallel job fails `live (ubuntu-latest)`; `docs/TESTING.md` and `docs/LIVE.md` describe the new layout. | P0.13c | DONE (#123) | P0.13a | Logging and diagnostics fixes from the manager's review of #103 to #112 (20 November 2026). Each was checked against the code. (1) **Secrets in the diagnostics ring.** `ferroan_diag::log_malformed` (`crates/ferroan-diag/src/lib.rs`) puts a hex excerpt of the raw body into the ring through `take_log` at any log level, and redaction cannot match hex. A malformed `/agent` CreateAgent body (`crates/ferroan-region/src/simulation.rs`) or an oversize UseCircuitCode (`crates/ferroan-client/src/circuit.rs`) puts session ids, the secure session id, the circuit code and the seed capability path into `/diag`, `diag dump` files and crash reports. Keep the excerpt out of the ring and the dump files: it goes only to the debug log line, and only after the known secret fields of that message type are masked; the ring keeps route, length and the parse error. (2) **Redaction list without a bound.** `SECRETS` in `crates/ferroan-log/src/redact.rs` gains four entries per login and is never pruned, and every log line scans all of them under one mutex. Forget a session's secrets when it closes, bound the list with a limit id in `docs/LIMITS.md`, and report a hit through the diagnostics helper. (3) **Robust HTTP metrics.** `record` in `crates/ferroan-robust/src/status.rs` keys rows by the raw path and stops adding at 128, so unauthenticated junk paths fill the table and real routes stop being counted. Label by the matched route (unmatched requests share one `other` row), not the raw path. (4) **Remote console sessions.** `crates/ferroan-log/src/console.rs` never expires a session. `RemoteConsole.cs` at the pin drops a connection idle for more than 500 seconds (`DoExpire`); do the same, and bound the number of sessions with a limit id. An empty `ConsolePass` accepting an empty `PASS` matches C# and stays. (5) **Structured fields dropped.** `EventFields::store` in `crates/ferroan-log/src/layer.rs` keeps only `message` and `agent_id`, so `error = %e` and fields from dependencies vanish from every output. Keep every field: appended as `key=value` in text and as JSON members, redacted like the message. (6) **Smaller ones.** The `ferroan_region_udp_unacked` gauge is only set in the resend branch and is not cleared when a circuit ends; make it the sum over open circuits. `Budget::charge` in `crates/ferroan-llsd/src/lib.rs`, and `drop_bound` in the reliability code, report the failing charge rather than the total that crossed the bound as `observed`. `catalog_ids()` and `static_limit()` in `crates/ferroan-diag/src/lib.rs` re-list the catalogue by hand; count `hit.id` directly so a new limit cannot be missed. Acceptance: a unit test for each, including a malformed CreateAgent body whose session id appears in no ring line, `diag dump` or panic file; `cargo xtask check`; the live job passes. Next action: the manager splits this row and keeps part (1) first. The proposed split is under Q13c in WORK_QUEUE.md. No code was changed. ## Phase 2: The region

| P2.0a2 | DONE | P2.0d | Circuit fixes from the review of #20 (18 November 2026). The reviewer read a Tranquillity checkout that was not the pin, so confirm each finding at the pin first, and record here any that turn out to be wrong. (1) `RegionHandshake` sends what `LLClientView.SendRegionHandshake` sends, filled from the region's configuration: the `RegionInfo4` block (`RegionFlagsExtended`, `RegionProtocols`), `CacheID`, `SimName`, `SimAccess`, `WaterHeight`, `ProductName` and the rest. Today `RegionInfo4` is empty and those fields are defaults. (2) Immediate `PacketAck`s take reliable sequence numbers. They must not, if C# sends them outside the sequence (`SendAckImmediate`). (3) A UDP receive or send error for one peer must not end the circuit. That includes `ConnectionReset` on Windows, which `OpenSimUDPBase` avoids by turning `SIO_UDP_CONNRESET` off. (4) A reliable packet that has used up its resends stays in the unacked window, and once 256 are stuck every later send fails with `TooManyUnacked`. Drop it, or drop the client as C# does. (5) The agent, session and circuit-code checks (`session_packet`) run, but their result is thrown away. Act on them, or remove them. (6) Send `LogoutReply` as C# does: reliable or not, and whether the server waits before closing. (7) Move the reliability code shared with `tools/bot/src/circuit.rs` into one place. (8) Bring the `circuit.rs` header to PORTING.md form at the pin. Acceptance: `RegionHandshake`, `PacketAck` sequencing and `LogoutReply` match a C# region's capture byte for byte, with IDs folded as in P0.10d, and `cargo xtask live run login grid --region ferroan` still passes. Pin review: `SendAckImmediate` bypasses the sequence counter only for initial/pending `UseCircuitCode`; established ACKs allocate ordinary sequences and batch up to 255 IDs. `LogoutReply` was already reliable and zero-coded, and C# closes without waiting for its ACK; new captured-byte tests prove that behavior. C# has no four-retry cutoff, instead retrying until client inactivity; Ferroan keeps its bounded four-retry policy but now removes exhausted entries, as this row permits. Discarded movement/throttle/height circuit-code stubs were removed, and implemented packet agent/session checks now run before appended ACKs. Handshake configuration uses region INI name/type, configured estate owner and pinned fresh-region settings; persisted region/estate stores remain planned. `cargo xtask check` passed locally, including `initial_ack_and_configured_handshake_match_csharp_datagrams`, `queued_ack_matches_csharp_bytes_and_consumes_a_sequence`, `logout_send_path_matches_csharp_bytes_and_finishes_without_ack`, `receive_and_send_peer_errors_do_not_end_the_circuit`, `failed_reliable_send_is_retained_and_retried` and shared-window exhaustion tests. PR [#31](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/pull/31), ordinary CI [run 44275172507](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/44275172507) (Ubuntu 2m15s, macOS 3m24s, Windows 4m31s), live [run 44275174613](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/44275174613) (6m36s) on `ae8a0cf`. Local and CI `cargo xtask live run login grid --region ferroan` printed `login: pass`, `live: run passed`, `logout: LogoutReply` and `grid login http: register, QueryAccess, CreateAgent, deregister match C#`; CI printed `grid http: robust-private 9, robust-public 0, home 4, neighbour 0`. Final evidence-revision checks are required before auto-merge and linked in the PR. No viewer check. |

| P2.0b2 | DONE | P2.0b | Event queue as Tranquillity answers it. #21 answers an empty poll at once with 200 and an empty `events` array, and starts ids at 1. Tranquillity `EventQueueGetModule` at the pin holds the poll and answers `NoEvents`, a 502, when nothing arrives, and starts ids at a random value. A viewer that gets an immediate 200 may poll again at once, in a loop. Match Tranquillity's hold, timeout and 502, and its starting id. If not, record the divergence and its viewer evidence as a decision in docs/DECISIONS.md. Acceptance: a test against a C# capture of an empty poll, and the bot's `run_until_ready` still passes. Also from the review of #21 (18 November 2026), confirmed at the pin first: a seed body that is not LLSD XML gets 406 and should get 400; a request to the seed that is not a POST gets 405 and should get 404; the poll reply is `application/llsd+xml` where `GetEvents` sends `application/xml`; the event id wraps after `i32::MAX` and moves on every empty poll, where C# moves it only when events are delivered; seed array entries that are not strings are skipped, where C# reads every entry with `AsString`. Also remove the unused `DefaultBodyLimit` layer, and report a failed `axum::serve` instead of dropping it. Acceptance also includes captured C# fixtures for the seed reply and an empty poll, in place of tests that only decode our own encoder's output. **Evidence (18 November 2026, #32, `fix/event-queue-review`):** 22 client unit tests and three captured HTTP tests pass, including `held_empty_poll_matches_csharp_capture_and_ignores_poll_body`, `seed_string_and_uri_names_match_captured_csharp_replies`, `seed_errors_and_options_match_captured_csharp_replies`, `delivered_event_batch_matches_captured_csharp_bytes`, and the bot `run_until_ready` handshake test. Paused-clock tests cover ID stability/overflow, deadlines, close wakeups, batch advancement and bounds; three extractor tests and the full `cargo xtask check` pass. Fixtures in `fixtures/caps` preserve C# bytes with endpoint/identity substitutions. Local C#/Rust holds: 58036/58004 ms, both empty 502 `text/html`; Rust reports `logout: LogoutReply` and grid-control HTTP matching C#. [CI](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/44279431077) on `af5d91c`: Linux 2m21s, macOS 3m49s, Windows 4m36s; [live](https://github.com/KanikaBreed/Ferroan/actions/runs/44279430825) 9m51s, all passed without retries. CI holds 58055/58002 ms, event batches 2/0; Rust proxy counts 9/0/10/0 and registration/QueryAccess/CreateAgent/deregistration match C#. Review qualifications (OPTIONS and C# signed overflow/markers) and the older shared binary-parser gap are recorded in WORK_QUEUE.md. Queue reuse across crossings, other capabilities and viewer validation remain pending. Final documentation CI is linked in #32. |
